// SPDX-License-Identifier: MPL-2.0

use alloc::{
    boxed::Box,
    collections::BinaryHeap,
    sync::{Arc, Weak},
    vec::Vec,
};
use core::{
    sync::atomic::{AtomicBool, Ordering},
    time::Duration,
};

use ostd::sync::SpinLock;

use super::Clock;

/// A timeout, represented in one of the two ways.
pub enum Timeout {
    /// The timeout is reached _after_ the `Duration` time is elapsed.
    After(Duration),
    /// The timeout is reached _when_ the clock's time is equal to `Duration`.
    When(Duration),
}

/// A timer with periodic functionality.
///
/// Setting the timer will trigger a callback function upon expiration of
/// the set time. To enable its periodic functionality, users should set
/// its `interval` field with [`Timer::set_interval`]. By doing this,
/// the timer will use the interval time to configure a new timing after expiration.
pub struct Timer {
    interval: SpinLock<Duration>,
    timer_manager: Arc<TimerManager>,
    registered_callback: Box<dyn Fn() + Send + Sync>,
    timer_callback: SpinLock<Weak<TimerCallback>>,
}

impl Timer {
    /// Create a `Timer` instance from a [`TimerManager`].
    /// This timer will be managed by the `TimerManager`.
    ///
    /// Note that if the callback instructions involves sleep, users should put these instructions
    /// into something like `WorkQueue` to avoid sleeping during system timer interruptions.
    fn new<F>(registered_callback: F, timer_manager: Arc<TimerManager>) -> Arc<Self>
    where
        F: Fn() + Send + Sync + 'static,
    {
        Arc::new(Self {
            interval: SpinLock::new(Duration::ZERO),
            timer_manager,
            registered_callback: Box::new(registered_callback),
            timer_callback: SpinLock::new(Weak::default()),
        })
    }

    /// Set the interval time for this timer.
    /// The timer will be reset with the interval time upon expiration.
    pub fn set_interval(&self, interval: Duration) {
        *self.interval.disable_irq().lock() = interval;
    }

    /// Cancel the current timer's set timeout callback.
    pub fn cancel(&self) {
        let timer_callback = self.timer_callback.disable_irq().lock();
        if let Some(timer_callback) = timer_callback.upgrade() {
            timer_callback.cancel();
        }
    }

    /// Set the timer with a timeout.
    ///
    /// The registered callback function of this timer will be invoked
    /// when reaching timeout. If the timer has a valid interval, this timer
    /// will be set again with the interval when reaching timeout.
    pub fn set_timeout(self: &Arc<Self>, timeout: Timeout) {
        let expired_time = match timeout {
            Timeout::After(timeout) => {
                let now = self.timer_manager.clock.read_time();
                now + timeout
            }
            Timeout::When(timeout) => timeout,
        };

        let timer_weak = Arc::downgrade(self);
        let new_timer_callback = Arc::new(TimerCallback::new(
            expired_time,
            Box::new(move || interval_timer_callback(&timer_weak)),
        ));

        let mut timer_callback = self.timer_callback.disable_irq().lock();
        if let Some(timer_callback) = timer_callback.upgrade() {
            timer_callback.cancel();
        }
        *timer_callback = Arc::downgrade(&new_timer_callback);
        self.timer_manager.insert(new_timer_callback);
    }

    /// Return the current expired time of this timer.
    pub fn expired_time(&self) -> Duration {
        let timer_callback = self.timer_callback.disable_irq().lock().upgrade();
        timer_callback.map_or(Duration::ZERO, |timer_callback| timer_callback.expired_time)
    }

    /// Return the remain time to expiration of this timer.
    ///
    /// If the timer has not been set, this method
    /// will return `Duration::ZERO`.
    pub fn remain(&self) -> Duration {
        let now = self.timer_manager.clock.read_time();
        let expired_time = self.expired_time();
        if expired_time > now {
            expired_time - now
        } else {
            Duration::ZERO
        }
    }

    /// Return a reference to the [`TimerManager`] which manages
    /// the current timer.
    pub fn timer_manager(&self) -> &Arc<TimerManager> {
        &self.timer_manager
    }

    /// Returns the interval time of the current timer.

The wait path has a more serious cleanup hazard: after any wake that leaves status `Pending`, it removes every pending operation with the same PID. A single process can issue multiple blocked `semop` calls from different threads, so one timeout can silently cancel another thread’s operation.

fn interval_timer_callback(timer: &Weak<Timer>) {
    let Some(timer) = timer.upgrade() else {
        return;
    };

    (timer.registered_callback)();
    let interval = timer.interval.disable_irq().lock();
    if *interval != Duration::ZERO {
        timer.set_timeout(Timeout::After(*interval));
    }
}

/// `TimerManager` is used to create timers and manage their expiries. It holds a clock and can
/// create [`Timer`]s based on this clock.
///
/// These created `Timer`s will hold an `Arc` pointer to this manager, hence this manager
/// will be actually dropped after all the created timers have been dropped.
pub struct TimerManager {
    clock: Arc<dyn Clock>,
    timer_callbacks: SpinLock<BinaryHeap<Arc<TimerCallback>>>,
}

impl TimerManager {
    /// Create a `TimerManager` instance from a clock.
    pub fn new(clock: Arc<dyn Clock>) -> Arc<Self> {
        Arc::new(Self {
            clock,
            timer_callbacks: SpinLock::new(BinaryHeap::new()),
        })
    }

    fn insert(&self, timer_callback: Arc<TimerCallback>) {
        self.timer_callbacks
            .disable_irq()
            .lock()
            .push(timer_callback);
    }

    /// Check the managed timers, and if any have timed out,
    /// call the corresponding callback functions.
    pub fn process_expired_timers(&self) {
        let callbacks = {
            let mut timeout_list = self.timer_callbacks.disable_irq().lock();
            if timeout_list.len() == 0 {
                return;
            }

            let mut callbacks = Vec::new();
            let current_time = self.clock.read_time();
            while let Some(t) = timeout_list.peek() {
                if t.is_cancelled() {
                    // Just ignore the cancelled callback
                    timeout_list.pop();
                } else if t.expired_time <= current_time {
                    callbacks.push(timeout_list.pop().unwrap());
                } else {
                    break;
                }
            }
            callbacks
        };

        for callback in callbacks {
            (callback.callback)();
        }
    }

    /// Create an [`Timer`], which will be managed by this `TimerManager`.
    pub fn create_timer<F>(self: &Arc<Self>, function: F) -> Arc<Timer>
    where
        F: Fn() + Send + Sync + 'static,
    {
        Timer::new(function, self.clone())
    }
}

/// A `TimerCallback` can be used to execute a timer callback function.
struct TimerCallback {
    expired_time: Duration,
    callback: Box<dyn Fn() + Send + Sync>,
    is_cancelled: AtomicBool,
}

impl TimerCallback {
    /// Create an instance of `TimerCallback`.
    fn new(timeout: Duration, callback: Box<dyn Fn() + Send + Sync>) -> Self {
        Self {
            expired_time: timeout,
            callback,
            is_cancelled: AtomicBool::new(false),
        }
    }

    /// Cancel a `TimerCallback`. If the callback function has not been called,
    /// it will never be called again.
    fn cancel(&self) {
        self.is_cancelled.store(true, Ordering::Release);
    }

    // Return whether the `TimerCallback` is cancelled.
    fn is_cancelled(&self) -> bool {
        self.is_cancelled.load(Ordering::Acquire)
    }
}

impl PartialEq for TimerCallback {
    fn eq(&self, other: &Self) -> bool {
        self.expired_time == other.expired_time
    }
}

impl Eq for TimerCallback {}

impl PartialOrd for TimerCallback {
    fn partial_cmp(&self, other: &Self) -> Option<core::cmp::Ordering> {
        Some(self.cmp(other))
    }
}

impl Ord for TimerCallback {
    fn cmp(&self, other: &Self) -> core::cmp::Ordering {
        // We want `TimerCallback`s to be processed in ascending order of `expired_time`,
        // and the in-order management of `TimerCallback`s currently relies on a maximum heap,
        // so we need the reverse instruction here.
        self.expired_time.cmp(&other.expired_time).reverse()
    }
}
        true
    }

    fn do_wait(&self) {
        let has_woken = &self.has_woken;
        while !has_woken.swap(false, Ordering::Acquire) {
            scheduler::park_current(has_woken);
        }
    }

    fn close(&self) {
        // This must use `Ordering::Acquire`, although we do not care about the return value. See
        // the memory order explanation at the top of the file for details.
        let _ = self.has_woken.swap(true, Ordering::Acquire);
    }
}

#!/usr/bin/env bash

# SPDX-License-Identifier: MPL-2.0

#
# assemble_review.sh — deterministic merge of persona comment fragments into the review file body.
# No LLM judgement here:
# group by persona in a fixed order,
# sort by file then line within each group,
# drop exact duplicates *within a persona*,
# write the frontmatter,
# and leave a <!-- SUMMARY --> placeholder for the orchestrator's LLM summary step.
#
# Usage: assemble_review.sh [--overwrite] <meta-file> <frag-dir> <output-file>
#   meta-file: lines `mode=`, `base=` (diff) or `files=` (files), `head=`,
#              `branch=`, `date=`, `title=`
#   frag-dir:  one <persona>.json per persona, each a JSON array of comments:
#       {"file","line","grounding","severity","problem","fix","diff"}
#   --overwrite: replace <output-file> if it already exists (otherwise fail).
set -euo pipefail

overwrite=0
pos=()
for a in "$@"; do
    case "$a" in
        --overwrite) overwrite=1 ;;
        --*) echo "assemble_review.sh: unknown flag: $a" >&2; exit 2 ;;
        *) pos+=("$a") ;;
    esac
done
if [[ ${#pos[@]} -ne 3 ]]; then
    echo "usage: assemble_review.sh [--overwrite] <meta-file> <frag-dir> <output-file>" >&2
    exit 2
fi
meta="${pos[0]}"; fragdir="${pos[1]}"; out="${pos[2]}"

# Refuse to clobber an existing review unless --overwrite (design §3).
if [[ -e "$out" && $overwrite -eq 0 ]]; then
    echo "assemble_review.sh: refusing to overwrite existing $out (pass --overwrite)" >&2
    exit 1
fi

python3 - "$meta" "$fragdir" "$out" <<'PY'
import json, os, re, sys
meta_path, fragdir, out = sys.argv[1], sys.argv[2], sys.argv[3]

# A guideline short-name is a lowercase kebab identifier (e.g. `lock-ordering`) and
# renders as code — a citable rule. A bug's grounding is a short plain-language
# description (e.g. "Off by one") and renders as prose, so it never reads as a rule name.
KEBAB = re.compile(r"[a-z0-9]+(-[a-z0-9]+)*")
def grounding_tag(g):
    return "`%s`" % g if KEBAB.fullmatch(g) else g

meta = {}
for line in open(meta_path):
    line = line.strip()
    if "=" in line:
        k, v = line.split("=", 1)
        meta[k] = v

# Fixed persona order -> review section title.
# "Correctness" is the Development persona's section (a bare "## Development" reads oddly; see the design doc).
ORDER = [("maintainability", "Maintainability"),
         ("development",      "Correctness"),
         ("security",         "Security"),
         ("hardware",         "Hardware"),
         ("documentation",    "Documentation")]

def load(persona):
    p = os.path.join(fragdir, persona + ".json")
    if not os.path.exists(p):
        return []          # persona not activated / did not run: legitimately absent
    # An EXISTING fragment that is unparseable or not a JSON array means a pass
    # produced broken output. Failing closed (rather than dropping it) is essential:
    # this skill is recall-first, so a silently-discarded fragment is a silent
    # recall hole the benchmark/orchestrator would never notice.
    try:
        data = json.load(open(p))
    except Exception as e:
        sys.stderr.write(f"assemble: FATAL: unparseable fragment {p}: {e}\n")
        sys.exit(2)
    if not isinstance(data, list):
        sys.stderr.write(f"assemble: FATAL: fragment {p} is not a JSON array of comments\n")
        sys.exit(2)
    return data

L = ["---",
     f"date: {meta.get('date','')}",
     f"mode: {meta.get('mode','diff')}"]
if meta.get("base"):
    L.append(f"base: {meta['base']}")
if meta.get("files"):
    L.append(f"files: {meta['files']}")
L += [f"head: {meta.get('head','')}",
      f"branch: {meta.get('branch','')}"]
if meta.get("title"):
    # json.dumps yields a valid YAML double-quoted scalar (escapes embedded quotes).
    L.append("title: " + json.dumps(meta["title"]))
L += ["---", "", "# Summary", "", "<!-- SUMMARY -->", ""]

counts = {}
for persona, title in ORDER:
    # Dedup is per-persona: identical comments from two different personas are both kept,
    # in their own sections (design §4).
    seen = set()
    uniq = []
    for c in load(persona):
        # "Exact" duplicate: identical in EVERY field (a normalized full-object key),
        # so two comments that differ in grounding, severity, or diff are both kept.
        # A partial key would collapse distinct findings — a recall hole.
        key = json.dumps(c, sort_keys=True)
        if key in seen:
            continue
        seen.add(key)
        uniq.append(c)
    counts[persona] = len(uniq)
    if not uniq:
        continue
    # Sort by file, then line, then a stable text key
    # so co-located comments have a deterministic order regardless of pass output order.
    uniq.sort(key=lambda c: (str(c.get("file", "")),
                             int(c.get("line", 0) or 0),
                             str(c.get("grounding", "")),
                             str(c.get("problem", ""))))
    L += [f"## {title}", ""]
    for c in uniq:
        loc = "`%s`" % c.get("file", "?")
        if c.get("line"):
            loc += " line %s" % c["line"]
        L += [f"### {loc}", ""]
        diff = c.get("diff")
        if diff:
            L.append("> ```diff")
            L += ["> " + dl for dl in str(diff).splitlines()]
            L += ["> ```", ""]
        L.append("%s (%s): %s" % (grounding_tag(c.get("grounding", "issue")),
                                  c.get("severity", "major"),
                                  (c.get("problem", "") or "").strip()))
        L += ["", "**Fix.** %s" % (c.get("fix", "") or "").strip(), ""]

open(out, "w").write("\n".join(L).rstrip() + "\n")
sys.stderr.write("assemble: " + ", ".join(f"{p}={counts[p]}" for p, _ in ORDER) + "\n")
PY

    85	#[derive(Debug)]
    86	pub struct Semaphore {
    87	    val: SpinLock<i32>,
    88	    /// PID of the process that last modified semaphore.
    89	    /// - through semop with op != 0
    90	    /// - through semctl with SETVAL and SETALL
    91	    /// - through SEM_UNDO when task exit
    92	    latest_modified_pid: AtomicU32,
    93	    /// Pending alter operations. For each pending operation, it has `sem_op < 0`.
    94	    pending_alters: SpinLock<LinkedList<PendingOp>>,
    95	    /// Pending zeros operations. For each pending operation, it has `sem_op = 0`.
    96	    pending_const: SpinLock<LinkedList<PendingOp>>,
    97	    /// Last semop time.
    98	    sem_otime: AtomicU64,
    99	}
   100	
   101	impl Semaphore {
   102	    pub fn set_val(&self, val: i32, current_pid: Pid) -> Result<()> {
   103	        if !(0..SEMVMX).contains(&val) {
   104	            return_errno!(Errno::ERANGE);
   105	        }
   106	
   107	        let mut current_val = self.val.lock();
   108	        *current_val = val;
   109	
   110	        self.update_pending_ops(current_val, current_pid);
   111	        Ok(())
   112	    }
   113	
   114	    pub fn val(&self) -> i32 {
   115	        *self.val.lock()
   116	    }
   117	
   118	    pub fn last_modified_pid(&self) -> Pid {
   119	        self.latest_modified_pid.load(Ordering::Relaxed)
   120	    }
   121	
   122	    pub fn sem_otime(&self) -> Duration {
   123	        Duration::from_secs(self.sem_otime.load(Ordering::Relaxed))
   124	    }
   125	
   126	    pub fn pending_zero_count(&self) -> usize {
   127	        self.pending_const.lock().len()
   128	    }
   129	
   130	    pub fn pending_alter_count(&self) -> usize {
   131	        self.pending_alters.lock().len()
   132	    }
   133	
   134	    /// Notifies the semaphore that the semaphore sets it belongs to have been removed.
   135	    pub(super) fn removed(&self) {
   136	        let mut pending_alters = self.pending_alters.lock();
   137	        for pending_alter in pending_alters.iter_mut() {
   138	            pending_alter.status.set_status(Status::Removed);
   139	            pending_alter.waker.wake_up();
   140	        }
   141	        pending_alters.clear();
   142	
   143	        let mut pending_const = self.pending_const.lock();
   144	        for pending_const in pending_const.iter_mut() {
   145	            pending_const.status.set_status(Status::Removed);
   146	            pending_const.waker.wake_up();
   147	        }
   148	        pending_const.clear();
   149	    }
   150	
   151	    pub(super) fn new(val: i32) -> Self {
   152	        Self {
   153	            val: SpinLock::new(val),
   154	            latest_modified_pid: AtomicU32::new(current!().pid()),
   155	            pending_alters: SpinLock::new(LinkedList::new()),
   156	            pending_const: SpinLock::new(LinkedList::new()),
   157	            sem_otime: AtomicU64::new(0),
   158	        }
   159	    }
   160	
   161	    fn update_otime(&self) {
   162	        self.sem_otime.store(
   163	            RealTimeCoarseClock::get().read_time().as_secs(),
   164	            Ordering::Relaxed,
   165	        );
   166	    }
   167	
   168	    fn sem_op(&self, sem_buf: &SemBuf, timeout: Option<Duration>, ctx: &Context) -> Result<()> {
   169	        let mut val = self.val.lock();
   170	        let sem_op = sem_buf.sem_op;
   171	        let current_pid = ctx.process.pid();
   172	
   173	        let flags = IpcFlags::from_bits_truncate(sem_buf.sem_flags as u32);
   174	        if flags.contains(IpcFlags::SEM_UNDO) {
   175	            todo!()
   176	        }
   177	
   178	        // Operate val
   179	        let positive_condition = sem_op.is_positive();
   180	        let negative_condition = sem_op.is_negative() && sem_op.abs() as i32 <= *val;
   181	        let zero_condition = sem_op == 0 && *val == 0;
   182	
   183	        if positive_condition || negative_condition {
   184	            let new_val = val
   185	                .checked_add(i32::from(sem_op))
   186	                .ok_or(Error::new(Errno::ERANGE))?;
   187	            if new_val > SEMVMX {
   188	                return_errno!(Errno::ERANGE);
   189	            }
   190	
   191	            *val = new_val;
   192	            self.update_pending_ops(val, current_pid);
   193	            return Ok(());
   194	        } else if zero_condition {
   195	            return Ok(());
   196	        }
   197	        drop(val);
   198	
   199	        // Need to wait for the semaphore
   200	        if flags.contains(IpcFlags::IPC_NOWAIT) {
   201	            return_errno!(Errno::EAGAIN);
   202	        }
   203	
   204	        // Add current to pending list
   205	        let (waiter, waker) = Waiter::new_pair();
   206	        let status = Arc::new(AtomicStatus::new(Status::Pending));
   207	        let pending_op = PendingOp {
   208	            sem_buf: *sem_buf,
   209	            status: status.clone(),
   210	            waker: waker.clone(),

    23	
    24	/// Maximum number of semaphore sets.
    25	pub const SEMMNI: usize = 32000;
    26	/// Maximum number of semaphores per semaphore ID.
    27	pub const SEMMSL: usize = 32000;
    28	/// Maximum number of seaphores in all semaphore sets.
    29	pub const SEMMNS: usize = SEMMNI * SEMMSL;
    30	/// Maximum number of operations for semop.
    31	pub const SEMOPM: usize = 500;
    32	/// MAximum semaphore value.
    33	pub const SEMVMX: i32 = 32767;
    34	/// Maximum value that can be recorded for semaphore adjustment (SEM_UNDO).
    35	pub const SEMAEM: i32 = SEMVMX;

    37	#[derive(Debug)]
    38	pub struct SemaphoreSet {
    39	    /// Number of semaphores in the set
    40	    nsems: usize,
    41	    /// Semaphores
    42	    sems: Box<[Arc<Semaphore>]>,
    43	    /// Semaphore permission
    44	    permission: IpcPermission,
    45	    /// Creation time or last modification via `semctl`
    46	    sem_ctime: AtomicU64,
    47	}
    48	
    49	impl SemaphoreSet {
    50	    pub fn nsems(&self) -> usize {
    51	        self.nsems
    52	    }
    53	
    54	    pub fn get(&self, index: usize) -> Option<&Arc<Semaphore>> {
    55	        self.sems.get(index)
    56	    }
    57	
    58	    pub fn permission(&self) -> &IpcPermission {
    59	        &self.permission
    60	    }
    61

    63	        Duration::from_secs(self.sem_ctime.load(Ordering::Relaxed))
    64	    }

    68	            RealTimeCoarseClock::get().read_time().as_secs(),
    69	            Ordering::Relaxed,
    70	        );
    71	    }
    72	
    73	    fn new(key: key_t, nsems: usize, mode: u16, credentials: Credentials<ReadOp>) -> Result<Self> {
    74	        debug_assert!(nsems <= SEMMSL);
    75	
    76	        let mut sems = Vec::with_capacity(nsems);
    77	        for _ in 0..nsems {
    78	            sems.push(Arc::new(Semaphore::new(0)));
    79	        }
    80	
    81	        let permission =
    82	            IpcPermission::new_sem_perm(key, credentials.euid(), credentials.egid(), mode);
    83

    85	            nsems,

    87	            permission,
    88	            sem_ctime: AtomicU64::new(RealTimeCoarseClock::get().read_time().as_secs()),
    89	        })
    90	    }
    91	}

    93	impl Drop for SemaphoreSet {
    94	    fn drop(&mut self) {
    95	        for sem in self.sems.iter() {

    97	        }

   100	            .get()
   101	            .unwrap()

# Summary

<!-- SUMMARY -->

## Maintainability

### `commit 6ce50fab1 message`

> ```diff
> Refactor semaphore to support atomic semop
> ```

`imperative-subject` (nit): The subject refers to the `semop` interface as plain prose instead of formatting the identifier with backticks.

**Fix.** Use a subject like:
```text
Refactor semaphore to support atomic `semop`
```

### `kernel/src/ipc/semaphore/system_v/sem.rs` line 66

> ```diff
> 41 #[repr(u16)]
> 42 #[derive(Debug, TryFromInt, Clone, Copy)]
> 43 pub enum Status {
> ...
> 65 /// Pending atomic semop.
> 66 pub struct PendingOp {
> ```

`narrow-visibility` (minor): `PendingOp` and the related `Status` enum are declared `pub`, but the only new consumer outside `sem.rs` is the sibling `sem_set` module. Because `system_v::sem` is public, this exposes pending-queue internals to the whole kernel crate and makes future queue representation changes harder.

**Fix.** Restrict the types and their helper methods to the semaphore subsystem, for example `pub(super) struct PendingOp` and `pub(super) enum PendingOpStatus`, keeping only the accessors needed by `sem_set` visible as `pub(super)`.

### `kernel/src/ipc/semaphore/system_v/sem.rs` line 230

> ```diff
> 229 /// Update pending const and alter operations, ref: <https://elixir.bootlin.com/linux/v6.0.9/source/ipc/sem.c#L1029>
> 230 pub(super) fn do_smart_update(
> 231     inner: &mut SpinLockGuard<SemSetInner, PreemptDisabled>,
> 232     pending_op: &PendingOp,
> 233 ) -> LinkedList<PendingOp> {
> 234     let mut wake_queue = LinkedList::new();
> 235 
> 236     let (sems, pending_alter, pending_const) = inner.field_mut();
> ```

`information-hiding` (major): `do_smart_update` takes a `SpinLockGuard<SemSetInner, PreemptDisabled>` and immediately calls `inner.field_mut()`, so `sem.rs` has to know the exact storage layout of `SemSetInner` owned by `sem_set.rs`. That leaks the set's implementation details across the module boundary and couples the scheduling helpers to the lock and field layout.

**Fix.** Move the pending-operation update logic behind behavior-level methods on `SemSetInner` or `SemaphoreSet`, such as `wake_after_successful_semop` and `wake_after_setval`, and remove the tuple-returning `field_mut` interface.

### `kernel/src/ipc/semaphore/system_v/sem.rs` line 301

> ```diff
> 300 /// Iter the sops and return the flags (alter, dupsop)
> 301 fn get_sops_flags(pending_op: &PendingOp) -> (bool, bool) {
> 302     let mut alter = false;
> 303     let mut dupsop = false;
> 304     let mut dup = 0;
> ```

`bool-names` (minor): `get_sops_flags` returns positional booleans named `alter` and `dupsop`; `alter` is not assertion-style and `dupsop` is an abbreviation. The call site `let (alter, dupsop)` leaves readers to remember which flag is which.

**Fix.** Return a small named struct, for example `SopsFlags { has_alter_op, has_duplicate_sop }`, and use those assertion-style field names at the call site.

### `kernel/src/ipc/semaphore/system_v/sem_set.rs` line 81

> ```diff
> 80 impl SemaphoreSet {
> 81     pub fn pending_const_count(&self, sem_num: u16) -> usize {
> 82         let inner = self.inner.lock();
> 83         let pending_const = &inner.pending_const;
> ```

`accurate-names` (minor): `pending_const_count` uses `const` for operations waiting for a semaphore value of zero, while the syscall name is `SEM_GETZCNT` and the field comment says "Pending zeros operations." Callers have to translate between `const` and zero-wait semantics.

**Fix.** Use zero-based terminology consistently, for example rename `pending_const_count` to `pending_zero_count` and `pending_const` to `pending_zero`, or add a behavior-oriented wrapper with that name for the syscall path.

### `kernel/src/ipc/semaphore/system_v/sem_set.rs` line 143

> ```diff
> 143     pub fn get<T>(&self, sem_num: usize, func: &dyn Fn(&Semaphore) -> T) -> Result<T> {
> 144         let inner = self.inner();
> 145         Ok(func(
> 146             inner.sems.get(sem_num).ok_or(Error::new(Errno::EINVAL))?,
> 147         ))
> 148     }
> ```

`least-surprise` (minor): `SemaphoreSet::get` looks like a normal accessor, but it actually runs a callback while holding the set lock. That unusual API forces `sys_semctl` to import `Semaphore` and add local `sem_val`/`sem_pid` functions just to read fields, making the lock lifetime and interface intent harder to see.

**Fix.** Either rename the callback API to `with_sem` and make the lock-scoped behavior explicit, or add purpose-specific methods such as `sem_val` and `sem_latest_modified_pid` so syscall code does not depend on `Semaphore` directly.

## Correctness

### `kernel/src/ipc/semaphore/system_v/sem.rs` line 190

> ```diff
> if let Some(timeout) = timeout {
>     pending_op.waker = Some(waker.clone());
> 
>     let jiffies_timer = JIFFIES_TIMER_MANAGER.get().unwrap().create_timer(move || {
>         waker.wake_up();
>     });
>     jiffies_timer.set_timeout(Timeout::After(timeout));
> }
> ...
> waiter.wait();
> ```

Lost timeout (major): `jiffies_timer` is dropped at the end of the `if let Some(timeout)` block, before `waiter.wait()`. The timer queue only keeps a `Weak<Timer>`, so a blocked `semtimedop` with a timeout never gets the timeout wakeup and can sleep until some unrelated semaphore change instead of returning `EAGAIN`.

**Fix.** Keep the `Arc<Timer>` alive until after `waiter.wait()`, and cancel it when the operation completes normally or is removed. Add a regression where `semtimedop` waits on an unsatisfied semaphore with a short timeout and returns `EAGAIN`.

### `kernel/src/ipc/semaphore/system_v/sem.rs` line 329

> ```diff
> for op in pending_op.sops_iter() {
>     let sem = sems.get(op.sem_num as usize).ok_or(Errno::EFBIG)?;
>     let flags = IpcFlags::from_bits_truncate(op.sem_flags as u32);
>     result = sem.val();
> ...
> for op in pending_op.sops_iter() {
>     let sem = &mut sems[op.sem_num as usize];
>     if op.sem_op != 0 {
>         sem.val += i32::from(op.sem_op);
> ```

Incorrect cumulative validation (major): `perform_atomic_semop` reinitializes `result` from `sem.val()` for every `SemBuf`, so operations in the same atomic batch do not see earlier operations on the same semaphore. For example, with `sem0 == 1`, `sops = [{ sem_num: 0, sem_op: -1 }, { sem_num: 0, sem_op: -1 }]` passes validation twice against `1`, then the apply loop subtracts twice and leaves `sem0 == -1` instead of waiting or returning `EAGAIN`.

**Fix.** Validate against a temporary per-semaphore value table in array order, then commit those temporary values only after the whole batch succeeds. The same logic should handle duplicate `sem_num` entries, zero waits after earlier decrements, and overflow checks.

### `kernel/src/ipc/semaphore/system_v/sem.rs` line 336

> ```diff
> if op.sem_op == 0 && result != 0 {
>     if flags.contains(IpcFlags::IPC_NOWAIT) {
>         return_errno!(Errno::EAGAIN);
>     } else {
>         return Ok(false);
>     }
> }
> ```

Incomplete validation (major): `perform_atomic_semop` returns `Ok(false)` as soon as one operation would block, before validating the rest of `sops`. With a one-semaphore set where `sem0 == 0`, `sops = [{ sem_num: 0, sem_op: -1 }, { sem_num: 1, sem_op: 0 }]` is enqueued instead of failing with `EFBIG`; when `sem0` later becomes available, `update_pending_alter` treats the later `Err(EFBIG)` like `Ok(false)` and the waiter is left pending forever.

**Fix.** Before enqueueing, validate every `SemBuf` for non-waitable errors such as an out-of-range `sem_num` and unsupported flags. When rechecking queued operations, propagate fatal errors to the waiter instead of leaving the operation on `pending_alter`.

### `kernel/src/ipc/semaphore/system_v/sem.rs` line 352

> ```diff
> if flags.contains(IpcFlags::SEM_UNDO) {
>     todo!()
> }
> ```

Reachable panic (major): `todo!()` is reachable from the `semop` syscall whenever user space passes `SEM_UNDO`, so an unsupported semaphore flag can panic the kernel instead of returning an error or performing undo accounting.

**Fix.** Implement `SEM_UNDO` accounting, or reject it with a normal syscall error until it is implemented. Do not leave `todo!()` on a user-controlled syscall path.

### `kernel/src/ipc/semaphore/system_v/sem_set.rs` line 84

> ```diff
> pub fn pending_const_count(&self, sem_num: u16) -> usize {
>     let inner = self.inner.lock();
>     let pending_const = &inner.pending_const;
>     let mut count = 1;
> ...
> pub fn pending_alter_count(&self, sem_num: u16) -> usize {
>     let inner = self.inner.lock();
>     let pending_alter = &inner.pending_alter;
>     let mut count = 1;
> ```

Off by one (major): `pending_const_count` and `pending_alter_count` initialize `count` to `1`, so `SEM_GETZCNT` and `SEM_GETNCNT` report one waiter even when no pending operation references `sem_num`. They also accept a truncated `u16` from `semctl`, so an invalid `semnum` can return a count instead of `EINVAL`.

**Fix.** Start both counters at `0`, accept `sem_num` as `usize`, and validate it against `self.nsems` before counting. Count each pending operation once for the requested semaphore.

### `kernel/src/ipc/semaphore/system_v/sem_set.rs` line 126

> ```diff
> let mut wake_queue = LinkedList::new();
> if val == 0 {
>     wake_const_ops(sems, pending_const, &mut wake_queue);
> } else {
>     update_pending_alter(sems, pending_alter, pending_const, &mut wake_queue);
> }
> ```

Incomplete wakeup (major): When `SEM_SETVAL` sets a semaphore to `0`, `setval` only scans `pending_const`. A mixed atomic operation such as `[{ sem_num: 0, sem_op: 0 }, { sem_num: 1, sem_op: -1 }]` is stored in `pending_alter`; if `sem1` is already available and `SEM_SETVAL` makes `sem0` zero, the operation is now runnable but this branch never checks it, so the waiter remains asleep.

**Fix.** After `SEM_SETVAL`, run the smart update path for `pending_alter` as well as zero waiters. The wakeup logic must re-evaluate mixed atomic operations whose zero condition may have become true.

## Security

### `kernel/src/ipc/semaphore/system_v/sem.rs` line 153

> ```diff
>     // TODO: Support permission check
>     warn!("Semaphore operation doesn't support permission check now");
> 
>     let (alter, dupsop) = get_sops_flags(&pending_op);
> ```

`validate-at-boundaries` (major): `sem_op()` consumes syscall-derived `sem_id` and `sops`, but it only logs that permission checks are unsupported and then executes `perform_atomic_semop()`. An adversary who knows a semaphore id can perform wait/alter operations on a set even when `SemaphoreSet::permission()` should deny `READ` or `ALTER` access.

**Fix.** Validate permissions before executing or queueing the operation. Derive the required `PermissionMode` from `sops` (`READ` for `sem_op == 0`, `ALTER` for nonzero operations, both for mixed batches) and enforce it against the caller credentials and `SemaphoreSet::permission()`. Do not rely on `check_sem()` until it performs the actual permission check.

### `kernel/src/syscall/semctl.rs` line 83

> ```diff
>         IpcControlCmd::SEM_GETZCNT => {
>             let cnt: usize = check_and_ctl(semid, PermissionMode::READ, |sem_set| {
>                 Ok(sem_set.pending_const_count(semnum as u16))
>             })?;
> ```

`validate-at-boundaries` (major): `SEM_GETZCNT` no longer validates the user-supplied `semnum` against the semaphore set size. The `semnum as u16` cast silently truncates large values, so a hostile `semnum` such as `65536` is treated as semaphore `0` instead of failing with `EINVAL`.

**Fix.** Validate `semnum` as a `usize` before calling the count helper, or make `pending_const_count()` take `usize` and return `Result<usize>` after checking `semnum < self.nsems()`.

### `kernel/src/syscall/semctl.rs` line 90

> ```diff
>         IpcControlCmd::SEM_GETNCNT => {
>             let cnt: usize = check_and_ctl(semid, PermissionMode::READ, |sem_set| {
>                 Ok(sem_set.pending_alter_count(semnum as u16))
>             })?;
> ```

`validate-at-boundaries` (major): `SEM_GETNCNT` has the same unvalidated `semnum as u16` boundary truncation. A caller can pass an out-of-range `semnum` and have it wrapped to another semaphore number instead of being rejected.

**Fix.** Validate `semnum` as a `usize` before calling the count helper, or make `pending_alter_count()` take `usize` and return `Result<usize>` after checking `semnum < self.nsems()`.

## Documentation

### `kernel/src/syscall/semop.rs` line 66

> ```diff
> ```diff
> +    let mut semops = Vec::with_capacity(nsops);
>      for i in 0..nsops {
> -        let sem_buf = user_space.read_val::<SemBuf>(tsops + size_of::<SemBuf>() * i)?;
> -        sem_op(sem_id, &sem_buf, timeout, ctx)?;

> ```
> ```

`linux-compat-docs` (major): This change makes `semop`/`semtimedop` execute the whole `nsops` array through one atomic `sem_op` call instead of executing each `SemBuf` separately, which enhances a user-visible syscall behavior. The Linux compatibility documentation was not updated in the same change, so the documented syscall coverage remains stale for the newly supported atomic multi-operation semantics.

**Fix.** Update the Linux compatibility syscall coverage for `semop` and `semtimedop` to describe the newly supported atomic `nsops` behavior, including the relevant `.scml` coverage artifact if this tree has one for System V semaphore syscalls.

# Summary

The refactor moves `semop` toward the right shape by collecting the full `nsops` array and centralizing semaphore-set state under one set-level lock. That is the right direction for atomic System V semaphore operations, and several new helper boundaries make the intended scheduling paths easier to identify.

The highest-risk issues are in the new atomic execution path. `semtimedop` drops its timer before sleeping, duplicate operations in one batch validate against stale values, fatal errors in later operations can be hidden by an earlier wait, and `SEM_SETVAL` can miss mixed waiters that become runnable when a semaphore reaches zero. The syscall-facing count paths also now report incorrect values and accept truncated `semnum` values.

Structurally, the change exposes more semaphore internals than needed and leaks `SemSetInner` layout across module boundaries. Tightening those interfaces while fixing the correctness issues would make the implementation easier to keep aligned with Linux semantics.

## Maintainability

### `commit 6ce50fab1 message`

> ```diff
> Refactor semaphore to support atomic semop
> ```

`imperative-subject` (nit): The subject refers to the `semop` interface as plain prose instead of formatting the identifier with backticks.

**Fix.** Use a subject like:
```text
Refactor semaphore to support atomic `semop`
```

### `kernel/src/ipc/semaphore/system_v/sem.rs` line 66

> ```diff
> 41 #[repr(u16)]
> 42 #[derive(Debug, TryFromInt, Clone, Copy)]
> 43 pub enum Status {
> ...
> 65 /// Pending atomic semop.
> 66 pub struct PendingOp {
> ```

`validate-at-boundaries` (major): `sem_op()` consumes syscall-derived `sem_id` and `sops`, but it only logs that permission checks are unsupported and then executes `perform_atomic_semop()`. An adversary who knows a semaphore id can perform wait/alter operations on a set even when `SemaphoreSet::permission()` should deny `READ` or `ALTER` access.

**Fix.** Validate permissions before executing or queueing the operation. Derive the required `PermissionMode` from `sops` (`READ` for `sem_op == 0`, `ALTER` for nonzero operations, both for mixed batches) and enforce it against the caller credentials and `SemaphoreSet::permission()`. Do not rely on `check_sem()` until it performs the actual permission check.

### `kernel/src/syscall/semctl.rs` line 83

> ```diff
>         IpcControlCmd::SEM_GETZCNT => {
>             let cnt: usize = check_and_ctl(semid, PermissionMode::READ, |sem_set| {
>                 Ok(sem_set.pending_const_count(semnum as u16))
>             })?;
> ```

`validate-at-boundaries` (major): `SEM_GETZCNT` no longer validates the user-supplied `semnum` against the semaphore set size. The `semnum as u16` cast silently truncates large values, so a hostile `semnum` such as `65536` is treated as semaphore `0` instead of failing with `EINVAL`.

**Fix.** Validate `semnum` as a `usize` before calling the count helper, or make `pending_const_count()` take `usize` and return `Result<usize>` after checking `semnum < self.nsems()`.

### `kernel/src/syscall/semctl.rs` line 90

> ```diff
>         IpcControlCmd::SEM_GETNCNT => {
>             let cnt: usize = check_and_ctl(semid, PermissionMode::READ, |sem_set| {
>                 Ok(sem_set.pending_alter_count(semnum as u16))
>             })?;
> ```

`validate-at-boundaries` (major): `SEM_GETNCNT` has the same unvalidated `semnum as u16` boundary truncation. A caller can pass an out-of-range `semnum` and have it wrapped to another semaphore number instead of being rejected.

**Fix.** Validate `semnum` as a `usize` before calling the count helper, or make `pending_alter_count()` take `usize` and return `Result<usize>` after checking `semnum < self.nsems()`.

## Documentation

### `kernel/src/syscall/semop.rs` line 66

> ```diff
> +    let mut semops = Vec::with_capacity(nsops);
>      for i in 0..nsops {
> -        let sem_buf = user_space.read_val::<SemBuf>(tsops + size_of::<SemBuf>() * i)?;
> -        sem_op(sem_id, &sem_buf, timeout, ctx)?;
> +        semops.push(user_space.read_val::<SemBuf>(tsops + size_of::<SemBuf>() * i)?);
>      }
>  
> +    sem_op(sem_id, semops, timeout, ctx)?;
> ```

`linux-compat-docs` (major): This change makes `semop`/`semtimedop` execute the whole `nsops` array through one atomic `sem_op` call instead of executing each `SemBuf` separately, which enhances a user-visible syscall behavior. The Linux compatibility documentation was not updated in the same change, so the documented syscall coverage remains stale for the newly supported atomic multi-operation semantics.

**Fix.** Update the Linux compatibility syscall coverage for `semop` and `semtimedop` to describe the newly supported atomic `nsops` behavior, including the relevant `.scml` coverage artifact if this tree has one for System V semaphore syscalls.

# Summary

The refactor moves `semop` toward the right shape by collecting the full `nsops` array and centralizing semaphore-set state under one set-level lock. That is the right direction for atomic System V semaphore operations, and several new helper boundaries make the intended scheduling paths easier to identify.

The highest-risk issues are in the new atomic execution path. `semtimedop` drops its timer before sleeping, duplicate operations in one batch validate against stale values, fatal errors in later operations can be hidden by an earlier wait, and `SEM_SETVAL` can miss mixed waiters that become runnable when a semaphore reaches zero. The syscall-facing count paths also now report incorrect values and accept truncated `semnum` values.

Structurally, the change exposes more semaphore internals than needed and leaks `SemSetInner` layout across module boundaries. Tightening those interfaces while fixing the correctness issues would make the implementation easier to keep aligned with Linux semantics.

## Maintainability

### `commit 6ce50fab1 message`

> ```diff
> Refactor semaphore to support atomic semop
> ```

`imperative-subject` (nit): The subject refers to the `semop` interface as plain prose instead of formatting the identifier with backticks.

**Fix.** Use a subject like:
```text
Refactor semaphore to support atomic `semop`
```

### `kernel/src/ipc/semaphore/system_v/sem.rs` line 66

> ```diff
> 41 #[repr(u16)]
> 42 #[derive(Debug, TryFromInt, Clone, Copy)]
> 43 pub enum Status {
> ...
> 65 /// Pending atomic semop.
> 66 pub struct PendingOp {
> ```

`narrow-visibility` (minor): `PendingOp` and the related `Status` enum are declared `pub`, but the only new consumer outside `sem.rs` is the sibling `sem_set` module. Because `system_v::sem` is public, this exposes pending-queue internals to the whole kernel crate and makes future queue representation changes harder.

**Fix.** Restrict the types and their helper methods to the semaphore subsystem, for example `pub(super) struct PendingOp` and `pub(super) enum PendingOpStatus`, keeping only the accessors needed by `sem_set` visible as `pub(super)`.

### `kernel/src/ipc/semaphore/system_v/sem.rs` line 230

> ```diff
> 229 /// Update pending const and alter operations, ref: <https://elixir.bootlin.com/linux/v6.0.9/source/ipc/sem.c#L1029>
> 230 pub(super) fn do_smart_update(
> 231     inner: {prompt}mut SpinLockGuard<SemSetInner, PreemptDisabled>,
> 232     pending_op: {prompt}PendingOp,
> 233 ) -> LinkedList<PendingOp> {
> 234     let mut wake_queue = LinkedList::new();
> 235 
> 236     let (sems, pending_alter, pending_const) = inner.field_mut();
> ```

`information-hiding` (major): `do_smart_update` takes a `SpinLockGuard<SemSetInner, PreemptDisabled>` and immediately calls `inner.field_mut()`, so `sem.rs` has to know the exact storage layout of `SemSetInner` owned by `sem_set.rs`. That leaks the set's implementation details across the module boundary and couples the scheduling helpers to the lock and field layout.

**Fix.** Move the pending-operation update logic behind behavior-level methods on `SemSetInner` or `SemaphoreSet`, such as `wake_after_successful_semop` and `wake_after_setval`, and remove the tuple-returning `field_mut` interface.

### `kernel/src/ipc/semaphore/system_v/sem.rs` line 301

> ```diff
> 300 /// Iter the sops and return the flags (alter, dupsop)
> 301 fn get_sops_flags(pending_op: {prompt}PendingOp) -> (bool, bool) {
> 302     let mut alter = false;
> 303     let mut dupsop = false;
> 304     let mut dup = 0;
> ```

`bool-names` (minor): `get_sops_flags` returns positional booleans named `alter` and `dupsop`; `alter` is not assertion-style and `dupsop` is an abbreviation. The call site `let (alter, dupsop)` leaves readers to remember which flag is which.

**Fix.** Return a small named struct, for example `SopsFlags { has_alter_op, has_duplicate_sop }`, and use those assertion-style field names at the call site.

### `kernel/src/ipc/semaphore/system_v/sem_set.rs` line 81

> ```diff
> 80 impl SemaphoreSet {
> 81     pub fn pending_const_count({prompt}self, sem_num: u16) -> usize {
> 82         let inner = self.inner.lock();
> 83         let pending_const = {prompt}inner.pending_const;
> ```

`accurate-names` (minor): `pending_const_count` uses `const` for operations waiting for a semaphore value of zero, while the syscall name is `SEM_GETZCNT` and the field comment says "Pending zeros operations." Callers have to translate between `const` and zero-wait semantics.

**Fix.** Use zero-based terminology consistently, for example rename `pending_const_count` to `pending_zero_count` and `pending_const` to `pending_zero`, or add a behavior-oriented wrapper with that name for the syscall path.

### `kernel/src/ipc/semaphore/system_v/sem_set.rs` line 143

> ```diff
> 143     pub fn get<T>({prompt}self, sem_num: usize, func: {prompt}dyn Fn({prompt}Semaphore) -> T) -> Result<T> {
> 144         let inner = self.inner();
> 145         Ok(func(
> 146             inner.sems.get(sem_num).ok_or(Error::new(Errno::EINVAL))?,
> 147         ))
> 148     }
> ```

`least-surprise` (minor): `SemaphoreSet::get` looks like a normal accessor, but it actually runs a callback while holding the set lock. That unusual API forces `sys_semctl` to import `Semaphore` and add local `sem_val`/`sem_pid` functions just to read fields, making the lock lifetime and interface intent harder to see.

**Fix.** Either rename the callback API to `with_sem` and make the lock-scoped behavior explicit, or add purpose-specific methods such as `sem_val` and `sem_latest_modified_pid` so syscall code does not depend on `Semaphore` directly.

## Correctness

### `kernel/src/ipc/semaphore/system_v/sem.rs` line 190

> ```diff
> if let Some(timeout) = timeout {
>     pending_op.waker = Some(waker.clone());
> 
>     let jiffies_timer = JIFFIES_TIMER_MANAGER.get().unwrap().create_timer(move || {
>         waker.wake_up();
>     });
>     jiffies_timer.set_timeout(Timeout::After(timeout));
> }
> ...
> waiter.wait();
> ```

Lost timeout (major): `jiffies_timer` is dropped at the end of the `if let Some(timeout)` block, before `waiter.wait()`. The timer queue only keeps a `Weak<Timer>`, so a blocked `semtimedop` with a timeout never gets the timeout wakeup and can sleep until some unrelated semaphore change instead of returning `EAGAIN`.

**Fix.** Keep the `Arc<Timer>` alive until after `waiter.wait()`, and cancel it when the operation completes normally or is removed. Add a regression where `semtimedop` waits on an unsatisfied semaphore with a short timeout and returns `EAGAIN`.

### `kernel/src/ipc/semaphore/system_v/sem.rs` line 329

> ```diff
> for op in pending_op.sops_iter() {
>     let sem = sems.get(op.sem_num as usize).ok_or(Errno::EFBIG)?;
>     let flags = IpcFlags::from_bits_truncate(op.sem_flags as u32);
>     result = sem.val();
> ...
> for op in pending_op.sops_iter() {
>     let sem = {prompt}mut sems[op.sem_num as usize];
>     if op.sem_op != 0 {
>         sem.val += i32::from(op.sem_op);
> ```

Incorrect cumulative validation (major): `perform_atomic_semop` reinitializes `result` from `sem.val()` for every `SemBuf`, so operations in the same atomic batch do not see earlier operations on the same semaphore. For example, with `sem0 == 1`, `sops = [{ sem_num: 0, sem_op: -1 }, { sem_num: 0, sem_op: -1 }]` passes validation twice against `1`, then the apply loop subtracts twice and leaves `sem0 == -1` instead of waiting or returning `EAGAIN`.

**Fix.** Validate against a temporary per-semaphore value table in array order, then commit those temporary values only after the whole batch succeeds. The same logic should handle duplicate `sem_num` entries, zero waits after earlier decrements, and overflow checks.

### `kernel/src/ipc/semaphore/system_v/sem.rs` line 336

> ```diff
> if op.sem_op == 0 {prompt}{prompt} result != 0 {
>     if flags.contains(IpcFlags::IPC_NOWAIT) {
>         return_errno!(Errno::EAGAIN);
>     } else {
>         return Ok(false);
>     }
> }
> ```

Incomplete validation (major): `perform_atomic_semop` returns `Ok(false)` as soon as one operation would block, before validating the rest of `sops`. With a one-semaphore set where `sem0 == 0`, `sops = [{ sem_num: 0, sem_op: -1 }, { sem_num: 1, sem_op: 0 }]` is enqueued instead of failing with `EFBIG`; when `sem0` later becomes available, `update_pending_alter` treats the later `Err(EFBIG)` like `Ok(false)` and the waiter is left pending forever.

**Fix.** Before enqueueing, validate every `SemBuf` for non-waitable errors such as an out-of-range `sem_num` and unsupported flags. When rechecking queued operations, propagate fatal errors to the waiter instead of leaving the operation on `pending_alter`.

### `kernel/src/ipc/semaphore/system_v/sem.rs` line 352

> ```diff
> if flags.contains(IpcFlags::SEM_UNDO) {
>     todo!()
> }
> ```

Reachable panic (major): `todo!()` is reachable from the `semop` syscall whenever user space passes `SEM_UNDO`, so an unsupported semaphore flag can panic the kernel instead of returning an error or performing undo accounting.

**Fix.** Implement `SEM_UNDO` accounting, or reject it with a normal syscall error until it is implemented. Do not leave `todo!()` on a user-controlled syscall path.

### `kernel/src/ipc/semaphore/system_v/sem_set.rs` line 84

> ```diff
> pub fn pending_const_count({prompt}self, sem_num: u16) -> usize {
>     let inner = self.inner.lock();
>     let pending_const = {prompt}inner.pending_const;
>     let mut count = 1;
> ...
> pub fn pending_alter_count({prompt}self, sem_num: u16) -> usize {
>     let inner = self.inner.lock();
>     let pending_alter = {prompt}inner.pending_alter;
>     let mut count = 1;
> ```

Off by one (major): `pending_const_count` and `pending_alter_count` initialize `count` to `1`, so `SEM_GETZCNT` and `SEM_GETNCNT` report one waiter even when no pending operation references `sem_num`. They also accept a truncated `u16` from `semctl`, so an invalid `semnum` can return a count instead of `EINVAL`.

**Fix.** Start both counters at `0`, accept `sem_num` as `usize`, and validate it against `self.nsems` before counting. Count each pending operation once for the requested semaphore.

### `kernel/src/ipc/semaphore/system_v/sem_set.rs` line 126

> ```diff
> let mut wake_queue = LinkedList::new();
> if val == 0 {
>     wake_const_ops(sems, pending_const, {prompt}mut wake_queue);
> } else {
>     update_pending_alter(sems, pending_alter, pending_const, {prompt}mut wake_queue);
> }
> ```

Incomplete wakeup (major): When `SEM_SETVAL` sets a semaphore to `0`, `setval` only scans `pending_const`. A mixed atomic operation such as `[{ sem_num: 0, sem_op: 0 }, { sem_num: 1, sem_op: -1 }]` is stored in `pending_alter`; if `sem1` is already available and `SEM_SETVAL` makes `sem0` zero, the operation is now runnable but this branch never checks it, so the waiter remains asleep.

**Fix.** After `SEM_SETVAL`, run the smart update path for `pending_alter` as well as zero waiters. The wakeup logic must re-evaluate mixed atomic operations whose zero condition may have become true.

## Security

### `kernel/src/ipc/semaphore/system_v/sem.rs` line 153

> ```diff
>     // TODO: Support permission check
>     warn!("Semaphore operation doesn't support permission check now");
> 
>     let (alter, dupsop) = get_sops_flags({prompt}pending_op);
> ```

`validate-at-boundaries` (major): `sem_op()` consumes syscall-derived `sem_id` and `sops`, but it only logs that permission checks are unsupported and then executes `perform_atomic_semop()`. An adversary who knows a semaphore id can perform wait/alter operations on a set even when `SemaphoreSet::permission()` should deny `READ` or `ALTER` access.

**Fix.** Validate permissions before executing or queueing the operation. Derive the required `PermissionMode` from `sops` (`READ` for `sem_op == 0`, `ALTER` for nonzero operations, both for mixed batches) and enforce it against the caller credentials and `SemaphoreSet::permission()`. Do not rely on `check_sem()` until it performs the actual permission check.

### `kernel/src/syscall/semctl.rs` line 83

> ```diff
>         IpcControlCmd::SEM_GETZCNT => {
>             let cnt: usize = check_and_ctl(semid, PermissionMode::READ, |sem_set| {
>                 Ok(sem_set.pending_const_count(semnum as u16))
>             })?;
> ```

`validate-at-boundaries` (major): `SEM_GETZCNT` no longer validates the user-supplied `semnum` against the semaphore set size. The `semnum as u16` cast silently truncates large values, so a hostile `semnum` such as `65536` is treated as semaphore `0` instead of failing with `EINVAL`.

**Fix.** Validate `semnum` as a `usize` before calling the count helper, or make `pending_const_count()` take `usize` and return `Result<usize>` after checking `semnum < self.nsems()`.

### `kernel/src/syscall/semctl.rs` line 90

> ```diff
>         IpcControlCmd::SEM_GETNCNT => {
>             let cnt: usize = check_and_ctl(semid, PermissionMode::READ, |sem_set| {
>                 Ok(sem_set.pending_alter_count(semnum as u16))
>             })?;
> ```

`validate-at-boundaries` (major): `SEM_GETNCNT` has the same unvalidated `semnum as u16` boundary truncation. A caller can pass an out-of-range `semnum` and have it wrapped to another semaphore number instead of being rejected.

**Fix.** Validate `semnum` as a `usize` before calling the count helper, or make `pending_alter_count()` take `usize` and return `Result<usize>` after checking `semnum < self.nsems()`.

## Documentation

### `kernel/src/syscall/semop.rs` line 66

> ```diff
> +    let mut semops = Vec::with_capacity(nsops);
>      for i in 0..nsops {
> -        let sem_buf = user_space.read_val::<SemBuf>(tsops + size_of::<SemBuf>() * i)?;
> -        sem_op(sem_id, {prompt}sem_buf, timeout, ctx)?;
> +        semops.push(user_space.read_val::<SemBuf>(tsops + size_of::<SemBuf>() * i)?);
>      }
>  
> +    sem_op(sem_id, semops, timeout, ctx)?;
> ```

`linux-compat-docs` (major): This change makes `semop`/`semtimedop` execute the whole `nsops` array through one atomic `sem_op` call instead of executing each `SemBuf` separately, which enhances a user-visible syscall behavior. The Linux compatibility documentation was not updated in the same change, so the documented syscall coverage remains stale for the newly supported atomic multi-operation semantics.

**Fix.** Update the Linux compatibility syscall coverage for `semop` and `semtimedop` to describe the newly supported atomic `nsops` behavior, including the relevant `.scml` coverage artifact if this tree has one for System V semaphore syscalls.
